f_{1}=f_{2}\sim\xi\zeta\zeta\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
yields
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}\big(\raisebox{-6.45831pt}{\hbox{\hbox to31.29pt{\vbox to16.33pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-8.91011pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 35.15 0 C 35.15 4.19 31.75 7.59 27.56 7.59 C 23.37 7.59 19.97 4.19 19.97 0 C 19.97 -4.19 23.37 -7.59 27.56 -7.59 C 31.75 -7.59 35.15 -4.19 35.15 0 Z M 27.56 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{17.41727pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{{}}{}{{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.56 5.56 C 9.98 9.98 16.74 9.08 17.72 8.26}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.76607}{-0.64278}{0.64278}{0.76607}{12.80373pt}{5.96939pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{{}}{}{{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.09 -9.09 C 10.4 -10.4 19.12 -12.05 23.05 -6.45}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-0.7071}{0.7071}{-0.7071}{-0.7071}{6.56662pt}{-6.56662pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\big)(f_{1}\otimes f_{2})\sim\zeta\zeta\notin\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
,

•

A fortiori
, the action of
1
2
fails to preserve
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm Lie}:=\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}\cap\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
.

This defect can be cured by carefully restricting the space of graphs, as embodied in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a vector space.

•

The graded algebra of functions
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{((\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*})[1])}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{dGra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of
1
-directed graphs.

•

The graded subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of sourced
1
-directed graphs.

•

The graded subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{s}_{-1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of sinked
1
-directed graphs.

•

The graded subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{s}_{1,-1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of
1
-directed graphs being both sourced and sinked.

Proof.

Let us first consider the action of a graph containing a source vertex and let
f
denote the function decorating the source upon the action of
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then the differential operator acting on
f
is of the form
\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}f
. Assuming that
f
belongs to the subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
ensures that
f
is at least linear in
\zeta
(by definition of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
, cf. Section
2.1
). Hence the function obtained as the outcome of the action of a sourced graph on
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
either vanishes or is at least linear in
\zeta
, so that it belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
. The subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
is therefore closed under the action of sourced graphs. A similar reasoning shows that
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
is closed under the action of sinked graphs. It follows that the intersection
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm Lie}:=\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}\cap\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
is closed under the action of graphs possessing at least one source and one sink.
∎

As noted previously (see Section
3.1
) oriented graphs necessarily possess at least one source and one sink. Denoting collectively by
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}
the three subalgebras
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie}}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{((\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*})[1])}
, the previous lemma thus ensures that
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of oriented 1-directed graphs, i.e., the representation
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}
induces morphisms of operads
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}\colon\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}}
.
Since we are primarily interested in the cohomology of the associated graph complexes, it is sufficient for our purpose to resort to the smaller operad of oriented graphs as this is where the cohomology lies (cf. the third item of Theorem
3.1
).
Applying
\text{\rm Def}(\mathsf{Lie}\left\{-2\right\}\to\cdot)
on both sides of the morphism
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}
yields the following proposition:

Proposition 4.4
.

•

The morphism of operads
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}\colon\mathsf{dGra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{((\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*})[1])}}
induces a morphism of dg Lie algebras

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fGC}_{3},\delta,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]\big)\to\big({\rm\mathsf{CE}}\big(\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{((\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*})[1])}[2]\big),\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}\big).

•

The morphisms of operads
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}}
induce morphisms of dg Lie algebras

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3},\delta,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]\big)\to\big({\rm\mathsf{CE}}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}[2]),\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}\big),

(4.2)

where
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}
collectively denotes the subalgebras
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}},\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}},\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm Lie}}\!\subset\!\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{((\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*})[1])}
.

Proof.

The proposition follows straightforwardly from Lemma
4.3
together with the equivariance of
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}
.
∎

We denoted
{\rm\mathsf{CE}}(\mathfrak{g})
the Chevalley–Eilenberg cochain space (in the adjoint representation) associated with the vector space
\mathfrak{g}
while
\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}
stands for the Nijenhuis–Richardson bracket and
\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}}:=\left[\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Omega},\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}
for the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential associated with the graded Poisson bracket (
2.1
). In plain words, Proposition
4.4
states that the graph complex
\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
provides a universal model for the deformation theory of proto-Lie bialgebras while
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
can be seen as a universal model for the deformation theory of Lie-quasi, quasi-Lie and Lie bialgebras. This last fact combined with the cohomology computations reviewed in Section
3.2
yields the following well-known result:

Corollary 4.5
.

The Grothendieck–Teichmüller group acts via
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-automorphisms on the deformation complexes of Lie-quasi, quasi-Lie and Lie bialgebras.

Proof.

Going to the zeroth cohomology in (
4.2
) yields a map

H^{0}\big(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}|\delta\big)\to H^{0}\big({\rm\mathsf{CE}}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}}[2])|\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}}\big).

Hence any non-trivial zero degree cocycle
\gamma
in
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
is mapped to a
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-derivation
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\gamma)
of the dg Lie algebras
\big(\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Omega}\big)
, thus ensuring that
\exp\big({\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\gamma)\big)
is a
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-automorphism thereof. Therefore the pro-unipotent group
\exp\big(H^{0}\big(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}\big)\big)
acts on the deformation complexes
\big(\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Omega}\big)
via
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-automorphisms. Focusing on connected graphs, the second item of Theorem
3.1
ensures that
H^{0}(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\simeq H^{0}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{2})\simeq\mathfrak{grt}_{1}
, where the last equivalence is T. Willwacher’s theorem
[
71
]
. Exponentienting thus yields an action of the pro-unipotent Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}\equiv\exp(\mathfrak{grt}_{1})
on the deformation complexes
\big(\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm sub}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Omega}\big)
of Lie-quasi, quasi-Lie and Lie bialgebras via
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-automorphisms.
∎

This result is consistent with the known action of
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
(via the
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
-torsor of Drinfeld associators) on quantization maps for Lie bialgebras
[
20
,
25
,
28
,
50
,
51
,
66
]
and Lie-quasi bialgebras
[
19
,
61
]
.
In comparison, there is no such action of
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
on the deformation complex of proto-Lie bialgebras as the above
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-automorphisms become trivial when acting on
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{((\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*})[1])}
(consistently with the fact that
H^{0}(\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\simeq H^{0}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\simeq{\mathbb{K}}
, cf. Table
3
). In that sense, there seems to be no true “intermediate case” between the proto-Lie bialgebra case and the Lie bialgebra case since restricting to oriented graphs allows to preserve all three subalgebras at once. As we will see, this fact is in contradistinction with the “bialgebroid” case in which Lie-quasi and quasi-Lie bialgebroids provide a true intermediate case between proto-Lie and Lie bialgebroids.

4.3
Lie bialgebroids

We now move on to the main result of this note by introducing novel universal models for the deformation complexes of the family of variations on Lie bialgebroids reviewed in Section
2.2
and Appendix
A
. Let
E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
be a vector bundle and consider the graded symplectic manifold
\big({\mathscr{E}},\Omega\big)
, with
{\mathscr{E}}\equiv T^{*}[2]E[1]
, coordinatised by the set of homogeneous coordinates
\big\{\underset{0}{x^{\mu}},\underset{1}{\xi^{a}},\underset{1}{\zeta_{a}},\underset{2}{p_{\mu}}\big\}
. The algebra of functions on
{\mathscr{E}}
carries a natural action
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}\colon\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}}
of the operad
\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
of bi-directed graphs containing both black and red directions. The action
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}
is defined similarly as the action (
4.1
) where for each edge
e\in E_{\gamma}
connecting vertices labeled by integers
i
and
j
, the operator
\Delta_{e}
is defined as

\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi^{a}_{(i)}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{a}^{(j)}},

where the sub(super)scripts
(i)
or
(j)
indicate that the derivative acts on the
i
-th or
j
-th factor in the tensor product.
As in the Lie bialgebra case, we note that the operator
\Delta_{e}
has grading
|\Delta_{e}|=-2
, consistently with the grading of edges in
\mathsf{dGra}_{3}
, and that
\Delta_{e}\Delta_{e^{\prime}}=\Delta_{e^{\prime}}\Delta_{e}
as is consistent with the fact that edges are bosonic for
d
odd.

Similarly as in the Lie bialgebra case, the action of the 3-Gerstenhaber operad
\mathsf{Ger}_{3}
(also called
\mathsf{e}_{3}
) on the algebra of functions
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}
factors through
\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
as
\mathsf{Ger}_{3}\overset{i_{3}}{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\overset{{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{End}_{\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}}
where the embedding is explicitly given by the following action on generators
a_{1}\wedge a_{2},\big\{{a_{1}},{a_{2}}\big\}\in\mathsf{Ger}_{3}(2)
:

of morphisms of operads

{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\color[rgb]{0.5898,0.1953,0.2344}

{{}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-0.9961}{-0.08809}{0.08809}{-0.9961}{14.29521pt}{-7.74997pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }\lxSVG@closescope }{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\big)(f_{1}\otimes f_{2})=\partial_{\mu}f_{1}(x){}^{\nu|[a}\partial_{\nu}f_{2}(x){}^{\mu|b]}\,\zeta_{a}\zeta_{b}\notin\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}.

A fortiori, the previous graph fails to preserve the intersection
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}:=\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}\cap\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
.

Hence
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie}}
generically fail to be
\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebras. Similarly to the Lie bialgebra case, this apparent defect can be cured via a suitable restriction of the class of graphs, as performed in the following lemma:

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
be a vector bundle.

•

The graded algebra of functions
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of bi-directed graphs.

•

The graded subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}^{\text{\rm black}}
-algebra via the action of bi-directed graphs with a black source.

•

The graded subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}^{\text{\rm red}}
-algebra via the action of bi-directed graphs with a red source.

•

The graded subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie}}
is endowed with a structure of
\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
-algebra via the action of bi-directed graphs with a black source and a red source.

Proof.

We mimic the proof in the Lie bialgebra case by considering first the action of a graph containing a black source vertex. Letting
f
denote the function decorating the black source upon the action of
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}
, the differential operator acting on
f
is of the form
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}f
. Assuming that
f
belongs to the subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
ensures that
f
is at least linear either in
p
or
\zeta
(by definition of
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
, cf. Section
2.2
). Hence the function obtained as the outcome of the action of a black sourced graph on
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
cannot be of the form
\xi\cdots\xi
so that it belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
. The subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
is therefore closed under the action of black sourced graphs. A similar reasoning shows that
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
is closed under the action of red sourced graphs.
65
65

65

Note that graphs with (black or red) sinks preserve neither
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
nor
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
.
It follows that the intersection
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}:=\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}\cap\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
is closed under the action of graphs possessing both a black and red source.
∎

As in the Lie bialgebra case, one can make use of the inclusion
\mathsf{o}_{\bullet}\mathsf{d}_{\bullet}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\subset\mathsf{s}_{\bullet}\mathsf{d}_{\bullet}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
(see Section
3.1
) to extract from the previous lemma an action of (suitable) operads of multi-oriented graphs on the three subalgebras at hand. Explicitly, the subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
) is acted upon by the operad of bi-directed graphs with oriented black (resp. red) arrows, denoted
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}^{\text{\rm black}}
(resp.
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}^{\text{\rm red}}
) in the following.
66
66

66

\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{a}^{(i)}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi^{a}_{(j)}}
for
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
.

Crucially, preserving the intersection
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}:=\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}\cap\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}
requires to orient
both
directions, thus yielding an action of
\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}
on
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
. As pointed out in Section
3.2
(see Theorem
3.1
), the number of oriented colors (in contradistinction with the number of directed colors) is the relevant factor to compute the respective cohomology. From this simple observation will then follow that the Lie bialgebroid case differs essentially from the dual cases of Lie-quasi and quasi-Lie bialgebroids.
Applying
\text{\rm Def}(\mathsf{Lie}\left\{-2\right\}\to\cdot)
on both sides of
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}\colon\mathsf{o}_{\bullet}\mathsf{d}_{\bullet}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathcal{A}^{E}}
yields the following proposition:

Proposition 4.8
.

•

The morphism of operads
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}\colon\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}}
induces a morphism of dg Lie algebras

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathsf{fGC}_{3},\delta,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]\big)\to\big({\rm\mathsf{CE}}\big(\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}[2]\big),\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}\big).

(4.3)

•

The morphisms of operads
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}\colon\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi}}}
induce morphisms of dg Lie algebras

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fGC}_{3},\delta,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]\big)\to\big({\rm\mathsf{CE}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi}}[2]\big),\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}\big),

(4.4)

where
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi}}
stands for
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm quasi-Lie}}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\infty}{({\mathscr{E}})}
.

•

The morphism of operads
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}\colon\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{Gra}_{3}\rightarrow\mathsf{End}_{\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie}}}
induces a morphism of dg Lie algebras

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3},\delta,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]\big)\to\big({\rm\mathsf{CE}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie}}[2]\big),\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}\big).

Proof.

The proposition is a direct consequence of Lemma
4.7
and the equivariance of the morphism
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}
.
∎

The conventions used are as in the bialgebra case and
\delta_{\rm\mathsf{S}}:=\big[\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{E}_{\Omega},\cdot\big]_{{\mathsf{NR}}}
stands for the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential associated with the graded Poisson bracket (
2.8
). Going into cohomology and using Theorem
3.1
allows to compute the relevant cohomology groups, as summed up in Table
4
.

Table 4:

The morphism (
4.3
) on the deformation complex of proto-Lie bialgebroids can be seen as a particular subcase of the action of
\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
on the deformation complex of Courant algebroids
[
53
]
when restricted to the split case.
67
67

67

Here by split Courant algebroids we mean Courant algebroids whose underlying vector bundle is a Whitney sum
E\oplus E^{*}
.
The latter does not yield interesting structures in degrees 0 and 1 as the dominant level of the relevant cohomology
H^{\bullet}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})
is located in degree
-3
. The corresponding cohomology space
H^{-3}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})
is a unital commutative algebra spanned by trivalent graphs modulo the
IHX relation
where the rôle of the unit is played by the
\Theta
-graph (see, e.g.,
[
4
]
). Given a proto-Lie bialgebroid
E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
represented by the Hamiltonian function
{\mathscr{H}}

(
see (
2.9
)
)
, each trivalent graph
\gamma\in H^{-3}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})
yields a cocycle function
\Omega_{\gamma}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)

(
i.e., such that
\big\{{{\mathscr{H}}},{\Omega_{\gamma}}\big\}_{\Omega}^{E}=0
)
thus yielding a conformal flow on the space of proto-Lie bialgebroids on
E
(cf.
[
53
]
for details).

Turning to Lie-quasi and quasi-Lie bialgebroids, the morphism (
4.4
) yields a natural extension of the action of the Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\exp\big(H^{0}(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\big)\simeq\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
on the deformation complexes of Lie-quasi and quasi-Lie bialgebras to the “bialgebroid” case. This is the essence of Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The proof is identical to the one of Corollary
4.5
upon substituting
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
with
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
, both admitting the same cohomology thanks to the first item of Theorem
3.1
.
∎

We refer to Section
4.4
for a discussion of the consequences of this action in the context of deformation quantization. Explicitly, the action of
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
is through graphs with one oriented color (either black or red) and as such generically
fails
to preserve the sub-deformation complex
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
for Lie bialgebroids. This is in contradistinction with the Lie bialgebra case whose deformation complex
\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm Lie}
does carry a representation of
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
. Rather the action of
\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
endows
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
with a new
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure deforming non-trivially the big bracket (
2.8
), as captured by Theorem
1.1
, for which we are now in position to articulate the following proof:

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

It follows from the third item of Proposition
4.8
that Maurer–Cartan elements for the dg Lie algebra
\big(\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3},\delta,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]\big)
are mapped via
{\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}
to universal deformations of the graded Lie algebra
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{E}_{\Omega}\big)
as a
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra. As recalled in Section
3.2
, applying the second item of Theorem
3.1
results in a sequence of isomorphisms

H^{1}(\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\!\simeq\!H^{1}(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\!\simeq\!H^{1}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{1})\!\simeq\!{\mathbb{K}}
,
thus providing a non-trivial cocycle graph
\Theta_{3}\!\in\!H^{1}(\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})
of degree
1
. The latter can be recursively extended to a non-trivial Maurer–Cartan element

\Upsilon_{\Theta_{3}}:=\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\hbox{\hbox to37.07pt{\vbox to8.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.30888pt\lower-4.30888pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.69 0 C 5.69 3.14 3.14 5.69 0 5.69 C -3.14 5.69 -5.69 3.14 -5.69 0 C -5.69 -3.14 -3.14 -5.69 0 -5.69 C 3.14 -5.69 5.69 -3.14 5.69 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 45.06 0 C 45.06 3.14 42.51 5.69 39.37 5.69 C 36.23 5.69 33.68 3.14 33.68 0 C 33.68 -3.14 36.23 -5.69 39.37 -5.69 C 42.51 -5.69 45.06 -3.14 45.06 0 Z M 39.37 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{25.95276pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

Remark 4.9
.

•

In the next section, we will consider Maurer–Cartan elements in the formal extension
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}{\vbox{\hbox{{$[[\hbar]]$}}}}
of
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
by a formal parameter
\hbar
. By analogy with the
d=2
case
[
50
]
, we will refer to Maurer–Cartan elements of
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}{\vbox{\hbox{{$[[\hbar]]$}}}},\theta\big)
as
formal “quantizable Lie bialgebroids”
, to contrast with the Maurer–Cartan elements of
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}{\vbox{\hbox{{$[[\hbar]]$}}}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{E}_{\Omega}\big)
referred to simply as
formal Lie bialgebroids
. Formal Lie bialgebroids being linear in
\hbar
are just Lie bialgebroids and accordingly, we will refer to formal “quantizable Lie bialgebroids” linear in
\hbar
as “quantizable Lie bialgebroids”.

•

Note that “quantizable Lie bialgebroids” are in particular Lie bialgebroids as they satisfy
\big\{{{\mathscr{H}}},{{\mathscr{H}}}\big\}_{\Omega}^{E}=0
on top of some higher consistency conditions
(
\theta_{6}\big({\mathscr{H}}^{\wedge 6}\big)=0
, etc.
)
.

The distinction between Lie bialgebroids and “quantizable Lie bialgebroids” will become salient when applied to the quantization problem for Lie bialgebroids in Section
4.4
.

4.4
Application to quantization and future directions

The quantization problem for Lie-(quasi) bialgebras was formulated by V. Drinfeld (cf.
[
18
, Question 1.1]
for Lie bialgebras and Section 5 for Lie-quasi bialgebras) and solved in
[
20
]
by Etingof--Kazhdan for the Lie bialgebra case
72
72

72

See also
[
25
,
28
,
50
,
51
,
66
]
.
and in
[
19
,
61
]
for the Lie-quasi bialgebra case. In both cases, the solution is universal and involves the use of a Drinfeld associator, yielding an action of the Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\mathsf{GRT}_{1}
on the set of inequivalent universal quantization maps. The latter can be traced back to the action of
H^{0}(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\simeq\mathfrak{grt}_{1}
on the deformation complex of Lie and Lie-quasi bialgebras, as reviewed in Section
4.2
. The situation for Lie bialgebras (and their quasi versions) is therefore much akin to the situation for finite-dimensional Poisson manifolds, in that both cases share the following important features (cf. the Introduction and Table
1
):

1.

The Grothendieck–Teichmüller group plays a classifying rôle.

2.

There is (conjecturally) no generic obstruction to the existence of universal quantizations.

As for Lie bialgebroids, the corresponding quantization problem was formulated by P. Xu in
[
74
,
76
]
as follows: Given a Lie bialgebroid structure on
(E,E^{*})
, the associated quantum object is a topological deformation (called
quantum groupoid
) of the standard (associative) bialgebroid structure on the universal enveloping algebra
\mathcal{U}_{R}(E)
associated with the Lie algebroid structure on
E

[
52
]
– with
R\equiv\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
– whose semi-classicalisation reproduces the original Lie bialgebroid structure. The quantization problem for Lie bialgebroids then consists in associating to each Lie bialgebroid a quantum groupoid quantizing it. Although the quantization problem for a generic Lie bialgebroid remains open, several explicit examples of quantizations for particular Lie bialgebroids have been exhibited in the literature. Apart from the above mentioned quantization of Lie bialgebras
[
20
]
, it was shown in
[
76
]
that M. Kontsevich’s solution to the quantization problem for (finite-dimensional) Poisson manifolds
[
34
]
ensures that Lie bialgebroids associated with Poisson manifolds (cf. Example
A.2
) constitute another example of Lie bialgebroids admitting a quantization.
73
73

73

More precisely, the Kontsevich star product
*
quantizing the Poisson bivector
\pi
provides a Drinfeld twistor
J_{*}\in\big(\mathcal{U}_{R}(E)\otimes_{R}\mathcal{U}_{R}(E)\big){\vbox{\hbox{{$[[\hbar]]$}}}}
for the standard bialgebroid
\mathcal{U}_{R}(E)
, where
R\equiv\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
and
E\equiv T\mathscr{M}
. Twisting
\mathcal{U}_{R}(E)
by
J_{*}
then provides a quantization of the Lie bialgebroid associated with
\pi
on
(T\mathscr{M},T^{*}\mathscr{M})
, see
[
76
]
.
This result was shown to extend to regular triangular Lie bialgebroids in
[
76
]
(see also
[
55
]
) using methods à la Fedosov
[
21
]
and to generic triangular Lie bialgebroids in
[
6
]
using a generalisation of Kontsevich’s formality theorem for Lie algebroids. In this context, the following natural conjecture was formulated by P. Xu:

Conjecture 4.10

(Xu
[
76
, Section 6]
)
.

Every Lie bialgebroid admits a quantization as a quantum groupoid.

Although Conjecture
4.10
might still hold true in the most general setting, we would like to argue for the non-existence of
universal
74
74

74

Recall that a universal quantization admits formulae given by expansions in terms of graphs with universal coefficients.
quantizations of Lie bialgebroids, on the basis of the following results from Section
4.3
:

1.

The Grothendieck–Teichmüller group plays no classifying rôle regarding the universal deformations (and hence quantizations) of Lie bialgebroids.

2.

There exists a potential obstruction to the existence of universal quantizations of Lie bialgebroids.

Contrasting these two features with their above mentioned counterparts for Lie bialgebras, one is led to conclude that the quantization problem for Lie bialgebroids differs essentially from its Lie bialgebra analogue and is in fact more akin to the quantization problem for infinite-dimensional manifolds. In view of this analogy, the obstruction appearing in Theorem
1.1
can be understood as the avatar in
d=3
of the Kontsevich–Shoikhet obstruction in
d=2
. As shown in
[
10
,
56
,
65
,
70
]
, the latter obstruction is hit in
d=2
and thus prevents the existence of an
oriented
star product, thereby yielding a No-go result regarding the existence of universal quantizations for infinite-dimensional Poisson manifolds. Pursuing the analogy with the
d=2
case, we conjecture the following:

Conjecture 4.11

(no-go)
.

There are no universal quantizations of Lie bialgebroids as quantum groupoids.

To explicitly show that the obstruction is hit would require a better understanding of the deformation theory of (associative) bialgebroids, which goes beyond the ambition of the present note.
75
75

75

While the deformation theory for associative algebras
[
23
]
and bialgebras
[
49
]
are well understood using the frameworks of operads and properads respectively, the deformation theory for bialgebroids has for now been evading such (pr)operadic formulation. The underlying reason is that the relevant category to deal with bialgebroids is the one of
\big(R^{e},R^{e}\big)
-bimodules – where
R
is a ring and
R^{e}
denotes its enveloping ring
R^{e}:=R\otimes R^{\text{op}}
– which is
not
symmetric monoidal, as usually required to work with (pr)operads. Rather, the category of
\big(R^{e},R^{e}\big)
-bimodules is naturally endowed with a structure of lax-oplax duoidal category (more precisely, a lax-strong duoidal category, that is the oplax structure is strong monoidal) whose bimonoids are bialgebroids, see, e.g.,
[
5
]
. As such, the deformation theory for bialgebroids cannot be described using the theory of properads (at least in its standard form). We are grateful to T. Basile and D. Lejay for clarifications regarding this fact.
We nevertheless conclude the present discussion by outlining a strategy of proof for Conjecture
4.11
by mimicking the two-steps procedure of
[
50
]
for Poisson manifolds and Lie bialgebras and adapting it to the case at hand. Denoting
C^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{GS}}(\mathcal{O}_{E},\mathcal{O}_{E}\big)
the equivalent of the Gerstenhaber–Schack complex for the standard commutative co-commutative bialgebroid structure on the symmetric algebra
\mathcal{O}_{E}
associated to the vector bundle
E
, the former should be endowed with a
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra structure
\mu
– generalising the one of
[
43
,
49
]
for the bialgebra case – whose corresponding Maurer–Cartan elements are quantum groupoids. By analogy with the bialgebra case, the cohomology of
\big(C^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{GS}}(\mathcal{O}_{E},\mathcal{O}_{E}),\mu_{1}\big)
should be isomorphic as a graded Lie algebra to the deformation complex
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{E}_{\Omega}\big)
of Lie bialgebroids on
E
. Although these two
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebras should coincide in cohomology, we do not expect them to be quasi-isomorphic as
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebras, i.e.,
\big(C^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{GS}}(\mathcal{O}_{E},\mathcal{O}_{E}\big),\mu\big)
is
not
formal. To show explicitly that the obstruction to formality is hit would require computing the
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra structure obtained by transfer of
\mu
on
H^{\bullet}\big(C^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{GS}}(\mathcal{O}_{E},\mathcal{O}_{E}),\mu_{1}\big)
and showing that the latter coincides with the exotic
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure
\theta
on
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
,
76
76

76

This is only possible thanks to the fact that the exotic
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure
\theta
on
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
of Theorem
1.1
is minimal and hence is a potential candidate for being the cohomology of another
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure.
as is the case in
d=2

[
50
,
65
]
. This would provide a trivial (in the sense that no Drinfeld associator is needed) formality
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphism
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie},\theta\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(C^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{GS}}(\mathcal{O}_{E},\mathcal{O}_{E}),\mu\big)
, yielding in turn a quantization map for (formal) “quantizable Lie bialgebroids” (the Maurer–Cartan elements of
\theta
in
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}{\vbox{\hbox{{$[[\hbar]]$}}}}
, cf. Remark
4.9
). Finally, the fact that
\theta
is not
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-isomorphic to the big bracket in
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
(cf. Theorem
1.1
) would prevent the existence of a formality morphism for Lie bialgebroids. We sum up these (non)-formality conjectures for Lie bialgebroids in Figure
6
.

Note that the situation is markedly different in the Lie-quasi (and quasi-Lie) bialgebroid case.
77
77

77

For definiteness, we will focus on the Lie-quasi case, keeping in mind that the arguments apply similarly to the dual case.
Firstly, recall that the Maurer–Cartan element
\Upsilon_{\Theta_{3}}
is
not

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{S}}
in
\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
– since
\Theta_{3}

\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
– hence
\theta

\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}

\Theta_{3}

\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}

\vartheta_{3}\in\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
such that
\Theta_{3}=-\delta\vartheta_{3}\in\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
. In order for
\Upsilon_{\Theta_{3}}
and
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{S}}
to be gauge-related in
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
, one needs to find a degree
0
element
78
78

78

The graph
\vartheta_{3}^{p}
has
N=4p+1
vertices and
k=6p
edges so that to have degree 0 in
d=3
.

\vartheta=\vartheta_{3}+\vartheta_{3}^{2}+\cdots+\vartheta_{3}^{p}
of
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
such that
\Upsilon_{\Theta_{3}}=e^{\text{\rm ad}\,\vartheta}\Upsilon_{\mathsf{S}}
. Contrarily to the problem of prolongating the cocycle
\Theta_{3}
to the Maurer–Cartan element
\Upsilon_{\Theta_{3}}
– which can be solved by a trivial induction – to display an explicit gauge map

is a highly non-trivial task
79
79

79

We refer to
[
50
]
for an explicit construction in
d=2
.
as the higher obstructions
80
80

80

The first obstruction vanishes since
\Theta_{3}
is exact in
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3}
. The second obstruction
\Upsilon^{2}_{\Theta_{3}}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\vartheta_{3},\Theta_{3}\right]
can be checked to live in
H^{1}(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})
.
live in
H^{1}(\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fcGC}_{3})\simeq H^{1}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{2})
, i.e., the recipient of the obstructions to the universal quantization of finite-dimensional Poisson manifolds. Although this cohomological space conjecturally vanishes
81
81

81

3
.
(Drinfeld–Kontsevich), maps allowing to convert cocycles into coboundaries are highly non-trivial and necessarily involve the choice of a Drinfeld associator (consistently with the fact that two coboundaries differ by the choice of an element in
H^{0}(\mathsf{fcGC}_{2})\simeq\mathfrak{grt}_{1}
). Up to the Drinfeld–Kontsevich conjecture, it is nevertheless expected that, given a Drinfeld associator, one can define a
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-isomorphism
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{E}_{\Omega}\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}},\theta\big)
. Repeating the argument laid down in the Lie bialgebroid case, one needs to find a (trivial)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphism
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{Lie-quasi}},\theta\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(C^{\bullet}_{\text{\rm quasi}-\mathsf{GS}}(\mathcal{O}_{E},\mathcal{O}_{E}),\mu\big)
, where the right-hand side stands for the deformation complex of the quantum object associated to Lie-quasi bialgebroids. The relevant category here is the one of
quasi-bialgebroids
, a common generalisation of the notions of bialgebroids and quasi-bialgebras, allowing to define the associated notion of
quasi-quantum groupoid
as topological deformation of the standard bialgebroid
\mathcal{U}_{R}(E)
as a quasi-bialgebroid. Composing with the (non-trivial)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-isomorphism
\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}^{E}_{\Omega}\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\big(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{\rm Lie-quasi}},\theta\big)
would yield a formality morphism for Lie-quasi bialgebroids. We sum up the discussion by formulating the following conjecture and recap the corresponding conjectural formality maps in the Lie-quasi case in Figure

.

Conjecture 4.12

(yes-go)
.

Given a Drinfeld associator, one can define a universal quantization of Lie-quasi bialgebroids as quasi-quantum groupoids.
