A dual conjecture can be made about quasi-Lie bialgebroids. Of particular interest in this context would be to investigate the quantization of quasi-Lie bialgebroids associated to twisted Poisson manifolds (cf. Example
A.4
) by means of a Drinfeld twistor
[
76
]
for the corresponding non-associative Kontsevich star product
[
7
]
.

To conclude, let us note that particularising the conjectural quantization map of Conjecture
4.12
to Lie bialgebroids ensures that every Lie bialgebroid admits a quantization
as a quasi-quantum groupoid
(but generically not as a quantum groupoid as stated in Conjecture
4.10
). However, for the particular subclass of “quantizable Lie bialgebroids” (which are in particular Lie bialgebroids, see Remark
4.9
) – such as Lie bialgebras and coboundary Lie bialgebroids (cf. Appendix
B
) – the associated quantization should yield a quantum groupoid. According to the above picture, the exotic
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure
\theta
of Theorem
1.1
can therefore be seen as a concrete means to delineate the subclass of Lie bialgebroids susceptible to be quantized as quantum groupoids.

Appendix A
Geometry of Lie bialgebroids

Lie bialgebras.

A Lie bialgebra is a vector space
\mathfrak{g}
endowed with a Lie algebra structure on both
\mathfrak{g}
and its dual
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
such that the cobracket
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}
is a cocycle for the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathfrak{g}})
, i.e.,
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}(\left[x,y\right])=\text{\rm ad}_{x}\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}(y)-\text{\rm ad}_{y}\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)
where the representation used is the extension
\text{\rm ad}:\mathfrak{g}\otimes(\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g})\to\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}
of the adjoint action of
(\mathfrak{g},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathfrak{g}})
on
\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}
as
\text{\rm ad}_{x}(y\wedge z)=\left[x,y\right]_{\mathfrak{g}}\wedge z+y\wedge\left[x,z\right]_{\mathfrak{g}}
. Letting
\left\{e_{a}\right\}|_{a\in\left\{1,\dots,\dim\mathfrak{g}\right\}}
be a basis of
\mathfrak{g}
, one denotes
\left[e_{a},e_{b}\right]_{\mathfrak{g}}=f_{ab}{}^{c}e_{c}
and
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}(e_{c})=C_{c}{}^{ab}e_{a}\otimes e_{b}
. The three defining conditions of a Lie bialgebra read

\displaystyle f_{e[a}{}^{d}f_{bc]}{}^{e}=0,\qquad C_{d}{}^{e[a}C_{e}{}^{bc]}=0,\qquad f_{ab}{}^{e}C_{e}{}^{cd}-4f_{e[a}{}^{[c}C_{b]}{}^{d]e}=0,

(A.1)

where
f_{ab}{}^{c}=f_{[ab]}{}^{c}
and
C_{c}{}^{ab}=C_{c}{}^{[ab]}
where square brackets denote skewsymmetrisation.

Lie algebroids.
A Lie algebroid is a triplet
(E,\rho,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E})
where:

•

E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
is a vector bundle over the manifold
\mathscr{M}
,

•

\rho\colon E\to T\mathscr{M}
is a morphism of vector bundles called the
anchor
,
82
82

82

We will use the same symbol
\rho
to denote the induced map of sections
\rho\colon\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(T\mathscr{M})
.

•

\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E}\colon\Gamma(E)\otimes\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(E)
is a
{\mathbb{K}}
-bilinear map called the
bracket
,

such that the following conditions are satisfied for all
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
and
X,Y,Z\in\Gamma(E)
:

1.

skewsymmetry
:
\left[X,Y\right]_{E}=-\left[Y,X\right]_{E}
,

2.

Leibniz rule
:
\left[X,f\cdot Y\right]_{E}=\rho_{X}[f]\cdot Y+f\cdot\left[X,Y\right]_{E}
,

3.

Jacobi identity
:
\left[X,\left[Y,Z\right]_{E}\right]_{E}+\left[Y,\left[Z,X\right]_{E}\right]_{E}+\left[Z,\left[X,Y\right]_{E}\right]_{E}=0
.

The previous conditions ensure that the map
\rho\colon\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(T\mathscr{M})
defines a morphism of Lie algebras between the Lie algebra
(\Gamma(E),\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E})
and the Lie algebra of vector fields on
\mathscr{M}
, i.e.,
\rho_{\left[X,Y\right]_{E}}=\left[\rho_{X},\rho_{Y}\right]
for all
X,Y\in\Gamma(E)
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
(E,\rho,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E})
be a Lie algebroid.
The following statements hold:

1.

Let
\left\{x^{\mu}\right\}|_{\mu\in\left\{1,\dots,\dim\mathscr{M}\right\}}
be a set of coordinates of
\mathscr{M}
and
\left\{e_{a}\right\}|_{a\in\left\{1,\dots,\dim E\right\}}
be a basis of
\Gamma(E)
. Setting
\rho_{e_{a}}[f]=\rho_{a}{}^{\mu}(x)\partial_{\mu}f
and
\left[e_{a},e_{b}\right]_{E}=f_{ab}{}^{c}(x)e_{c}
, the defining conditions of a Lie algebroid can be expressed in components as

f_{ab}{}^{c}=-f_{ba}{}^{c},\qquad 2\rho_{[a}{}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\rho_{b]}{}^{\mu}=\rho_{c}{}^{\mu}f_{ab}{}^{c},\qquad\rho_{[c}{}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}f_{ab]}{}^{d}=f_{e[c}{}^{d}f_{ab]}{}^{e}.

Acting on generic sections of
E
, the Lie algebroid bracket reads

\left[X,Y\right]_{E}=\big(\rho_{X}[Y^{c}]-\rho_{Y}[X^{c}]+f_{ab}{}^{c}X^{a}Y^{b}\big)e_{c}.

2.

The exterior algebra
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})
is naturally endowed with a structure of dg commutative algebra with differential
{\rm d}_{E}\colon\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})\to\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet+1}E^{*})
defined by

•

({\rm d}_{E}f)(X)=\rho_{X}[f]
,

•

({\rm d}_{E}\eta)(X,Y)=\rho_{X}[\eta(Y)]-\rho_{Y}[\eta(X)]-\eta(\left[X,Y\right]_{E})
,

•

{\rm d}_{E}(\alpha\wedge\beta)=({\rm d}_{E}\alpha)\wedge\beta+(-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha\wedge({\rm d}_{E}\beta)
,

for all
X,Y\in\Gamma(E)
,
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
,
\eta\in\Gamma(E^{*})
and
\alpha,\beta\in\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})
.

3.

The dual exterior algebra
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)
is endowed with a structure of Gerstenhaber algebra with graded bracket
\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}_{E}\colon\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)\otimes\Gamma(\wedge^{\circ}E)\to\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet+\circ-1}E)
defined as follows

\displaystyle\big\{{f},{g}\big\}_{E}=0,\qquad\big\{{X},{f}\big\}_{E}=\rho_{X}[f],\qquad\big\{{X},{Y}\big\}_{E}=\left[X,Y\right]_{E},

\displaystyle\big\{{P},{Q}\big\}_{E}=-(-1)^{(|p|-1)(|q|-1)}\big\{{Q},{P}\big\}_{E},

\displaystyle\big\{{P},{Q\wedge R}\big\}_{E}=\big\{{P},{Q}\big\}_{E}\wedge R+(-1)^{|q|(|p|-1)}Q\wedge\big\{{P},{R}\big\}_{E},

for all
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
,
X,Y\in\Gamma(E)
and
P,Q,R\in\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)
.

These conditions can be checked to ensure the graded Jacobi identity:

\displaystyle\big\{{\big\{{P},{Q}\big\}_{E}},{R}\big\}_{E}+(-1)^{(|P|-1)(|Q|+|R|)}\big\{{\big\{{Q},{R}\big\}_{E}},{P}\big\}_{E}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+(-1)^{(|R|-1)(|P|+|Q|)}\big\{{\big\{{R},{P}\big\}_{E}},{Q}\big\}_{E}=0.

Example A.2
.

•

A Lie algebra is a Lie algebroid whose base manifold is a point.

•

Given a manifold
\mathscr{M}
, the
standard Lie algebroid
is defined as the tangent bundle
T\mathscr{M}
together with the identity map as anchor and the usual Lie bracket of vector fields as bracket. The corresponding differential on the space of differential forms
\Omega^{\bullet}(\mathscr{M})\simeq\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}\mathscr{M})
coincides with the
de Rham differential
while the induced Gerstenhaber bracket on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}T\mathscr{M})
identifies with the
Schouten bracket
on polyvector fields.

•

Let
(\mathscr{M},\pi)
be a Poisson manifold. The dual tangent bundle
T^{*}\mathscr{M}
is naturally endowed with a Lie algebroid structure with anchor
\pi^{\sharp}\colon\Gamma(T^{*}\mathscr{M})\to\Gamma(T\mathscr{M})\colon\alpha_{\mu}{\rm d}x^{\mu}\mapsto\pi^{\mu\nu}\alpha_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}
and bracket
\left[\alpha,\beta\right]_{\pi}=\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\pi^{\sharp}(\alpha)}\beta-\mathcal{L}_{\pi^{\sharp}(\beta)}\alpha+i_{\pi^{\sharp}(\alpha)}{\rm d}\beta-i_{\pi^{\sharp}(\beta)}{\rm d}\alpha\big)\text{ for all }\alpha,\beta\in\Gamma(T^{*}\mathscr{M})
.

Lie bialgebroids.
The concept of Lie bialgebroid was introduced by Mackenzie–Xu in
[
42
]
as the infinitesimal variant of a Poisson groupoid. We follow the modern definition of
[
36
]
and define a Lie bialgebroid as a vector bundle
E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
endowed with two dual Lie algebroid structures satisfying a natural compatibility condition. Denoting
(\rho,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E})
the Lie algebroid structure on
E
and
(R,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E^{*}})
the one on
E^{*}
, the pair
(E,E^{*})
is a Lie bialgebroid if
{\rm d}_{E^{*}}
is a derivation of
\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}_{E}
, where we denoted
\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}_{E}
the Gerstenhaber bracket on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)
induced by
(\rho,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E})
and
{\rm d}_{E^{*}}
the differential on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)
induced by
(R,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E^{*}})
.
83
83

83

Note that the defining condition of a Lie bialgebroid can equivalently be stated as the fact that the differential
{\rm d}_{E}
on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})
induced by
(\rho,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E})
is a derivation of the Gerstenhaber bracket
\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}_{E^{*}}
induced by
(R,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E^{*}})
, i.e., the notion of Lie bialgebroid is self-dual.

Example A.3
.

•

A Lie bialgebra is a Lie bialgebroid whose base manifold is a point.

•

Letting
\mathscr{M}
be a Poisson manifold, the two Lie algebroid structures on
T\mathscr{M}
and
T^{*}\mathscr{M}
as defined in Example
A.2
are compatible in the above sense and hence define a Lie bialgebroid structure on
(T\mathscr{M},T^{*}\mathscr{M})
.

Lie bialgebroids thus generalise both Lie bialgebras and Poisson manifolds. In fact, the base manifold
\mathscr{M}
of any Lie bialgebroid is endowed with a canonical Poisson bracket
84
84

84

More generally, for a proto-Lie bialgebroid, the Jacobi identity for the bivector is deformed as

\pi^{\rho[\lambda}\partial_{\rho}\pi^{\mu\nu]}=\tfrac{1}{3}R^{a[\lambda}R^{b|\mu}R^{c|\nu]}\psi_{abc}+\tfrac{1}{3}\rho_{a}{}^{[\lambda}\rho_{b}{}^{\mu}\rho_{c}{}^{\nu]}\varphi^{abc}.

defined as
\big\{{f},{g}\big\}=\left\langle{\rm d}_{E^{*}}f,{\rm d}_{E}g\right\rangle

(
or in components as
\big\{{f},{g}\big\}=R^{a[\mu}\rho_{a}{}^{\nu]}\partial_{\mu}f\partial_{\nu}g
)
.

The exterior algebra
\big(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E),\wedge\big)
of a Lie bialgebroid is endowed with both a structure of Gerstenhaber bracket
\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}_{E}
and of a differential
d_{E^{*}}
being a derivation for both the graded commutative product
\wedge
and the Gerstenhaber bracket. Such a quadruplet
\big(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E),\wedge,{\rm d}_{E^{*}},\left\{{\cdot},{\cdot}\right\}_{E}\big)
is called a strong differential Gerstenhaber algebra. It was in fact shown in
[
75
]
(see also
[
36
]
) that Lie bialgebroid structures on a vector bundle
E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
are in one-to-one correspondence with strong differential Gerstenhaber structures on
\big(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E),\wedge\big)

(
or equivalently with dg Poisson structures on
(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1],\wedge)

)
.

Coboundary Lie bialgebroids.

Letting
E
be a Lie algebroid, an
r
-matrix
is a section
\Lambda\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E\big)
satisfying
\big\{{X},{\big\{{\Lambda},{\Lambda}\big\}_{E}}\big\}_{E}=0
for all
X\in\Gamma(E)
. An
r
-matrix endows
E
with a structure of Lie bialgebroid by defining
d_{E^{*}}=\big\{{\Lambda},{\cdot}\big\}_{E}
. The defining condition on
\Lambda
is necessary and sufficient to ensure that the inner derivation
d_{E^{*}}
squares to zero. A Lie bialgebroid defined in this way is called a coboundary Lie bialgebroid. Whenever the stronger condition
\big\{{\Lambda},{\Lambda}\big\}_{E}=0
holds, the induced Lie bialgebroid is said to be
triangular

[
42
]
(cf. for example the Lie bialgebroid on Poisson manifolds defined in Example
A.2
). If furthermore,
\Lambda
is of constant rank, the triangular Lie bialgebroid is said to be
regular
.

Quasi-Lie, Lie-quasi and proto-Lie bialgebroids.
The above mentioned characterisation of Lie bialgebroids as dg Poisson structures on
\big(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1],\wedge\big)
calls for several natural generalisations, as summarised in the following table
85
85

85

As is transparent from the correspondence below, there is a series of inclusions of bialgebroids: Lie
\subset
quasi-Lie
\subset
proto-Lie and Lie
\subset
Lie-quasi
\subset
proto-Lie. Note furthermore that the notions of Lie bialgebroids and proto-Lie bialgebroids are self-dual
(
and thus can be defined on both
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
and
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})[1]
)
while the notions of Lie-quasi bialgebroids and quasi-Lie bialgebroids are dual to each other.
(see, e.g.,
[
2
,
22
,
37
,
60
]
):

•

Lie bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
dg Poisson algebras on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
,

•

quasi-Lie bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
homotopy Poisson algebras
86
86

86

Recall that a (curved) homotopy Poisson structure on a graded commutative algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\wedge)
is a (curved)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure on
\mathfrak{g}
such that all brackets are multi-derivations with respect to
(\mathfrak{g},\wedge)
, see, e.g.,
[
38
,
44
]
. A dg Poisson algebra is thus a (flat) homotopy Poisson algebra for which the brackets of arity above 2 vanish.
on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
,

•

Lie-quasi bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
homotopy Poisson algebras on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})[1]
,

•

proto-Lie bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
curved homotopy Poisson algebras on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
.

We now focus on the most general case, namely proto-Lie bialgebroids. Apart from the usual data (two anchors
\rho
,
R
and two brackets
\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E}
,
\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{E^{*}})
, a proto-Lie bialgebroid contains two elements
\varphi\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{3}E\big)
and
\psi\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{3}E^{*}\big)
which play the rôle of various obstructions to the usual Lie bialgebroid identities. Letting
E^{*}[1]
be the shifted dual bundle coordinatised by
\{x^{\mu},\zeta_{a}\}
of respective degree
0
and
1
, the graded commutative algebra
\big(\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(E^{*}[1]\right),\cdot\big)
is isomorphic to the exterior algebra of sections
\big(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E),\wedge\big)
. The most general curved homotopy Poisson structure
l
on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
thus takes the form
87
87

87

The bracket
l_{p}
being of degree
3-2p
on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)\simeq\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(E^{*}[1]\right)
, the fact that each bracket is a multi-derivation for the underlying graded commutative algebra constrains all brackets of arity higher than 3 to vanish. Pulling back the brackets along the suspension map
s\colon\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]\to\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)
of degree 1 yields a series of brackets on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
with the usual degree
2-p
.

\displaystyle\bullet\ l_{0}:=\frac{1}{6}\varphi^{abc}\zeta_{a}\zeta_{b}\zeta_{c},

\displaystyle\bullet\ l_{1}(f):={\rm d}_{E^{*}}f=R^{a|\mu}\zeta_{a}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}-\frac{1}{2}C_{c}{}^{ab}\zeta_{a}\zeta_{b}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\zeta_{c}},

\displaystyle\bullet\ l_{2}(f,g):=\big\{{f},{g}\big\}_{E}=-\rho_{a}{}^{\mu}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial\zeta_{a}}\frac{\partial g}{\partial x^{\mu}}+(-1)^{|f|}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\zeta_{a}}\right)+(-1)^{|f|}f_{ab}{}^{c}\zeta_{c}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\zeta_{a}}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\zeta_{b}},

\displaystyle\bullet\ l_{3}(f,g,h):=(-1)^{|g|}\psi_{abc}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\zeta_{a}}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\zeta_{b}}\frac{\partial h}{\partial\zeta_{c}}.

Imposing the defining quadratic condition
\left[l,l\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}=0
of a (curved)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra yields a series of identities which precisely reproduce the components conditions (
2.10
)–(
2.18
) as

\displaystyle\bullet\ \left[l_{0},l_{1}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}=0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathcal{C}_{8}=0,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\bullet\ \left[l_{0},l_{2}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left[l_{1},l_{1}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}=0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathcal{C}_{3}=\mathcal{C}_{4}=0,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\bullet\ \left[l_{0},l_{3}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}+\left[l_{1},l_{2}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}=0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathcal{C}_{5}=\mathcal{C}_{6}=\mathcal{C}_{7}=0,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\bullet\ \left[l_{1},l_{3}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left[l_{2},l_{2}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}=0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathcal{C}_{1}=\mathcal{C}_{2}=0,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\bullet\ \left[l_{2},l_{3}\right]_{\mathsf{NR}}=0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathcal{C}_{9}=0.

(A.6)

Imposing
\psi\equiv 0
(resp.
\varphi\equiv 0
) yields a Lie-quasi (resp. quasi-Lie) bialgebroid and Lie bialgebroids are recovered by setting
\psi\equiv 0
,
\varphi\equiv 0
. Assuming that the base manifold
\mathscr{M}
is the one-point manifold and denoting the vector space
\Gamma(E)
as
\mathfrak{g}
allows to define the counterparts of these notions in the “bialgebra” realm, cf. Section
2.1
.

Twisting.
The previous formulation allows us to introduce the notion of
twist
of a proto-Lie bialgebroid in terms of twist of (curved)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebras.
88
88

88

The twisting procedure has been introduced by Quillen
[
57
]
for dg Lie algebras and later generalised by Getzler
[
24
]
to
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebras.
Letting
(\mathfrak{g},l)
be a nilpotent (curved)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra and
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathfrak{g}^{1}
be an arbitrary element of degree
1
, one defines the
twisted brackets

l^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n}\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\wedge n}\to\mathfrak{g}
of degree
2-n
for
n\geq 0
as

l^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n}(a_{1},\dots,a_{n}):=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{1}{k!}l_{k+n}(\mathfrak{m}^{\wedge k},a_{1},\dots,a_{n}),\qquad\text{where}\quad a_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}.

The twisted brackets can be checked to endow
\mathfrak{g}
with a structure of
curved

\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra. Whenever the curvature
l^{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\in\mathfrak{g}^{2}
vanishes, the element
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathfrak{g}^{1}
is called a
Maurer–Cartan element
and
(\mathfrak{g},l^{\mathfrak{m}})
is then a
flat

\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra.

Given a proto-Lie bialgebroid on
(E,E^{*})
defined by the curved homotopy Poisson algebra
\big(\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1],\wedge,l\big)
where
l:=\left\{l_{p}\right\}_{0\leq p\leq 3}
, one can define a new proto-Lie bialgebroid structure on
(E,E^{*})
via twisting the (curved)
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra structure
l
by an arbitrary bivector
\Lambda\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E\big)

(
being the most general element of degree
1
in
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
)
. In components, such a twist amounts to the following shift of the components of the proto-Lie bialgebroid:

\displaystyle\rho_{a}{}^{\mu}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}\rho_{a}{}^{\mu},\qquad f_{ab}{}^{c}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}f_{ab}{}^{c}+\psi_{abe}\Lambda^{ec},\qquad\psi_{abc}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}\psi_{abc},

\displaystyle R^{a|\mu}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}R^{a|\mu}-\rho_{b}{}^{\mu}\Lambda^{ba},\qquad C_{c}{}^{ab}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}C_{c}{}^{ab}+\rho_{c}{}^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\Lambda^{ab}+2\Lambda^{e[a}f_{ec}{}^{b]}-\psi_{cef}\Lambda^{ea}\Lambda^{fb},

\displaystyle\varphi^{abc}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}\varphi^{abc}-3R^{[c|\mu}\partial_{\mu}\Lambda^{ab]}+3\Lambda^{e[c}C_{e}{}^{ab]}+3\rho_{d}{}^{\mu}\Lambda^{d[a}\partial_{\mu}\Lambda^{bc]}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\varphi^{abc}\overset{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow}}{}-3f_{ef}{}^{[c}\Lambda^{e|a}\Lambda^{f|b]}-\psi_{def}\Lambda^{da}\Lambda^{eb}\Lambda^{fc}.

(A.7)

The latter can be checked to form the components of a proto-Lie bialgebroid without extra assumption on the bivector
\Lambda
. Note that, while a twist by a generic
\Lambda
does preserve the subspace of Lie-quasi bialgebroids (for which
\psi\equiv 0
), only bivectors satisfying the Maurer–Cartan equation
l_{1}(\Lambda)+\frac{1}{2}l_{2}(\Lambda,\Lambda)+\frac{1}{6}l_{3}(\Lambda,\Lambda,\Lambda)=0
in
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E)[1]
preserve the subspace of quasi-Lie bialgebroids
89
89

89

Equivalently, the Maurer–Cartan condition ensures that the twisting preserves the flatness of the
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-algebra associated to a given quasi-Lie bialgebroid, see footnote
88
.
(for which
\varphi\equiv 0
), see
[
40
]
for the Lie bialgebroid case and
[
60
]
for quasi-Lie bialgebroids.

Example A.4

(twisted Poisson manifolds)
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a manifold and
H\in\Omega^{3}(\mathscr{M})
be a closed 3-form.
There is a quasi-Lie bialgebroid structure
on
(T\mathscr{M},T^{*}\mathscr{M})
defined in components as

\displaystyle\rho_{\nu}{}^{\mu}=\delta_{\nu}{}^{\mu},\qquad f_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}=0,\qquad R^{\nu|\mu}=0,\qquad C_{\lambda}{}^{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\psi_{\lambda\mu\nu}=H_{\lambda\mu\nu}.

Letting
\pi\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}T\mathscr{M}\big)
be a bivector, the components of the twisted proto-Lie bialgebroid read

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{\nu}{}^{\mu}=\delta_{\nu}{}^{\mu},\qquad\tilde{f}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}=H_{\mu\nu\gamma}\pi^{\gamma\lambda},\qquad\tilde{R}^{\nu|\mu}=-\pi^{\mu\nu},\qquad\tilde{C}_{\lambda}{}^{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\lambda}\pi^{\mu\nu}-H_{\lambda\rho\sigma}\pi^{\rho\mu}\pi^{\sigma\nu},

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}^{\lambda\mu\nu}=3\,\pi^{\rho[\lambda}\partial_{\rho}\pi^{\mu\nu]}-H_{\rho\sigma\gamma}\pi^{\rho\lambda}\pi^{\sigma\mu}\pi^{\gamma\nu},\qquad\tilde{\psi}_{\lambda\mu\nu}=H_{\lambda\mu\nu}.

The resulting proto-Lie bialgebroid is again a quasi-Lie bialgebroid if and only
\tilde{\varphi}\equiv 0
, that is, if
\pi
is a twisted Poisson bivector
[
60
]
.
90
90

90

Recall that
twisted Poisson bivectors

[
31
,
64
]
are bivectors satisfying the twisted Jacobi identity
\left[\pi,\pi\right]_{{\mathsf{S}}}=\frac{1}{3}H(\pi,\pi,\pi)
.

Whenever
H
vanishes, we recover the Lie bialgebroid structure on the (co)-tangent bundle of a Poisson manifold, cf. Example
A.3
.

Dually, one can consider twisting the curved homotopy Poisson algebra structure on

\Gamma({\wedge}^{\bullet}\!E^{*}\!)[1]\!
by a 2-form field
\omega\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E^{*}\big)
which amounts to the following shift of the components of the proto-Lie bialgebroid

\displaystyle\rho_{a}{}^{\mu}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}\rho_{a}{}^{\mu}-R^{b|\mu}\omega_{ba},\qquad f_{ab}{}^{c}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}f_{ab}{}^{c}+R^{c|\mu}\partial_{\mu}\omega_{ab}-2\omega_{d[a}C_{b]}{}^{cd}-\varphi^{cde}\omega_{da}\omega_{eb},

\displaystyle R^{a|\mu}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}R^{a|\mu},\qquad C_{c}{}^{ab}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}C_{c}{}^{ab}+\varphi^{abd}\omega_{dc},\qquad\varphi^{abc}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}\varphi^{abc},

\displaystyle\psi_{abc}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}\psi_{abc}-3\rho_{[a}{}^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\omega_{bc]}+3\omega_{e[c}f_{ab]}{}^{e}+3\,R^{d|\mu}\omega_{d[a}\partial_{\mu}\omega_{bc]}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\psi_{abc}\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}}{}-3C_{[c}{}^{de}\omega_{d|a}\omega_{e|b}-\varphi^{def}\omega_{da}\omega_{eb}\omega_{fc}.

(A.8)

Twisting by a 2-form field does preserve quasi-Lie bialgebroids but fails to preserve Lie-quasi bialgebroids unless
\omega
satisfies the associated Maurer–Cartan equation on
\Gamma(\wedge^{\bullet}E^{*})[1]
.

Courant algebroids.
A Courant algebroid structure on a pseudo-Euclidean vector bundle
91
91

91

We remind the reader that a
pseudo-Euclidean vector bundle
is a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}\to\mathscr{M}
endowed with a symmetric, non-degenerate and
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
-bilinear form on the space of sections of
\mathcal{E}
, denoted
{\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\vee\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\to{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}(\mathscr{M})
and referred to as the
fiber-wise metric

\big({\mathcal{E}},{\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{E}}\big)
is a pair
\big(\rho_{\mathcal{E}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\big)
where

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\to\Gamma(T\mathscr{M})
is a
{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}(\mathscr{M})
-linear map called the
anchor
while
\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\otimes\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\to\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
is a
{\mathbb{K}}
-bilinear form on the fibers of
E
referred to as the
Dorfman bracket
.

The latter satisfy the following minimal set of axioms:

1.

The Dorfman bracket satisfies the Jacobi identity in its Leibniz form

\left[e_{1},\left[{e_{2}},{e_{3}}\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\right]_{\mathcal{E}}=\left[\left[e_{1},{e_{2}}\right]_{\mathcal{E}},{e_{3}}\right]_{\mathcal{E}}+\left[{e_{2}},\left[e_{1},{e_{3}}\right]\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\qquad\text{for all }e_{1},{e_{2}},{e_{3}}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E}).

2.

The symmetric part of the Dorfman bracket is controlled by the anchor

\left\langle\left[e_{1},e_{1}\right]_{\mathcal{E}},{e_{2}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}|_{e_{2}}\big[\left\langle e_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\big]\qquad\text{for all }e_{1},{e_{2}}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E}).

3.

The fiber-wise metric is compatible with the Courant algebroid structure

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}|_{e_{1}}\big[\left\langle{e_{2}},{e_{3}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\big]=\left\langle\left[e_{1},{e_{2}}\right]_{\mathcal{E}},{e_{3}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}+\left\langle{e_{2}},\left[e_{1},{e_{3}}\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\qquad\text{for all }e_{1},{e_{2}},{e_{3}}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E}).

Letting
\big({\mathcal{E}},{\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{E}},\rho_{\mathcal{E}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\big)
and
\big({\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},{\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},\rho_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\big)
be two Courant algebroids over the same manifold
92
92

92

More general notions of morphisms of Courant algebroids over different base manifolds can be defined, see
[
68
]
for a thorough treatment.

\mathscr{M}
, a morphism of Courant algebroids is defined as a morphism of the underlying vector bundles
\mathcal{F}\in\text{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
preserving the additional structures,
93
93

93

A morphism of vector bundles
\mathcal{F}\in\text{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
satisfying
\left\langle e_{1},e_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}=\left\langle\mathcal{F}(e_{1}),\mathcal{F}(e_{2})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
will be called a morphism of pseudo-vector bundles.
i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\langle e_{1},e_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}=\left\langle\mathcal{F}(e_{1}),\mathcal{F}(e_{2})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},\qquad\rho_{\mathcal{E}}=\rho_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\circ\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\left[e_{1},e_{2}\right]_{\mathcal{E}})=\left[\mathcal{F}(e_{1}),\mathcal{F}(e_{2})\right]_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\qquad\text{for all }e_{1},{e_{2}}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E}).

Whenever the morphism
\mathcal{F}
is invertible, it is called an isomorphism of Courant algebroids.

Courant algebroids first appeared implicitly in the work of I. Dorfman
[
15
]
and T. Courant
[
8
,
9
]
on integrable Dirac structures before their precise geometric structure was abstracted away in
[
40
]
to account for the concept of double of Lie bialgebroids.
More generally, the double
{\mathcal{E}}:=E\oplus E^{*}
of a proto-Lie bialgebroid
(E,E^{*})
carries a natural structure of pseudo-Euclidean vector bundle with fiber-wise metric
\left\langle e_{1},e_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}:=\alpha(Y)+\beta(X)
, where
e_{1}:=X\oplus\alpha
and
e_{2}:=Y\oplus\beta
for all
X,Y\in\Gamma(E)
and
\alpha,\beta\in\Gamma(E^{*})
. One can furthermore endow
{\mathcal{E}}
with the anchor
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}(e_{1}):=\rho(X)\oplus R(\alpha)
while the Dorfman bracket is defined through the following explicit expression

\displaystyle\left[e_{1},e_{2}\right]_{\mathcal{E}}:=\big(\left[X,Y\right]_{E}+\mathcal{L}^{E^{*}}_{\alpha}Y-\iota_{\beta}{\rm d}_{E^{*}}X-\varphi(\alpha,\beta,\cdot)\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\left[e_1,e_2 \right]_{\mathcal{E}}:=}{}\oplus\big(\left[\alpha,\beta\right]_{E^{*}}+\mathcal{L}^{E}_{X}\beta-\iota_{Y}d_{E}\alpha-\psi(X,Y,\cdot)\big),

where
\mathcal{L}^{E}_{X}
stands for the unique derivative operator extending the action of
\left[X,\cdot\right]_{E}
to the tensor algebra of
E

(
and similarly for
\mathcal{L}^{E^{*}}_{\alpha}
)
. The axioms of a proto-Lie bialgebroid thus ensure that the pair
\big(\rho_{\mathcal{E}},\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\big)
defines a Courant algebroid structure on
\big({\mathcal{E}},{\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{E}}\big)
, cf.
[
58
, Theorem 3.8.2]
.

Example A.5

(exact Courant algebroids)
.

A Courant algebroid such that the following sequence
94
94

94

The vector bundle morphism
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\colon T^{*}\mathscr{M}\to\mathcal{E}
is defined via
\left\langle\rho^{*}_{\mathcal{E}}|_{\alpha},e\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}=\left\langle\alpha,\rho_{\mathcal{E}}|_{e}\right\rangle
for all
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}\left(\mathscr{M}\right)
and
e\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
, and satisfies
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}=0
.

0\longrightarrow T^{*}\mathscr{M}\overset{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{E}\overset{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}{\longrightarrow}T\mathscr{M}\longrightarrow 0

is exact is called an exact Courant algebroid. It is an important result due to P. Ševera
[
63
]
that exact Courant algebroids over a fixed base manifold
\mathscr{M}
are classified
95
95

95

More precisely, any exact Courant algebroid is isomorphic to
\mathcal{E}:=T\mathscr{M}\oplus T^{*}\mathscr{M}
with canonical fiber-wise metric, projection
T\mathscr{M}\oplus T^{*}\mathscr{M}\to T\mathscr{M}
as anchor and Dorfman bracket
\left[e_{1},e_{2}\right]_{\mathcal{E}}:=\left[X,Y\right]\oplus\big(\mathcal{L}_{X}\beta-\iota_{Y}d\alpha-H(X,Y,\cdot)\big)
, where
H\in\Omega^{3}(\mathscr{M})
is a closed 3-form. The latter Courant algebroid structure coincides with the one induced by the quasi-bialgebroid structure of Example
A.4
. Letting
\omega\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E^{*}\big)
, the isomorphism
X\oplus\alpha\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}X\oplus\big(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\iota_{X}\omega\big)
corresponding to the twist (
2.20
) amounts to shift the closed 3-form
H
by an exact 3-form as
H\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}H-d\omega
. Hence isomorphism classes of exact Courant algebroids over
\mathscr{M}
are in bijective correspondence with elements of
H^{3}_{\textsf{dR}}(\mathscr{M})
.
by the third de Rham cohomology
H^{3}_{\textsf{dR}}(\mathscr{M})
of
\mathscr{M}
.

Dirac structures.

A subbundle
L\subset\mathcal{E}
of a Courant algebroid
\mathcal{E}
is called a Dirac structure if:

1.

L
is maximally isotropic with respect to the fiber-wise metric
{\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{E}}
, i.e.,
\left\langle\Gamma(L),\Gamma(L)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}=0
,
\dim L=\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathcal{E}
.

2.

The space of sections
\Gamma(L)
is closed under the Courant bracket
\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\mathcal{E}}
, i.e.,
\left[\Gamma(L),\Gamma(L)\right]_{\mathcal{E}}\subseteq\Gamma(L)
.

These conditions ensure in particular that the restrictions of the Courant anchor and bracket to
L
endow the vector bundle
L
with a structure of Lie algebroid.

Example A.6

(Dirac structure)
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a manifold and
[H]\in H^{3}_{\textsf{dR}}(\mathscr{M})
. Given a representative
H\in\Omega^{3}(\mathscr{M})
and a twisted Poisson structure
\pi
with respect to
H
, the graph of the map
\pi^{\sharp}\colon T^{*}\mathscr{M}\to T\mathscr{M}
can be checked to be a Dirac structure for the exact Courant algebroid associated to
\big(\mathscr{M},[H]\big)

[
63
,
64
]
.

A pair
(\mathcal{E},L)
where
L
is a Dirac structure for the Courant algebroid
\mathcal{E}
is referred to as a
Manin pair
. A triplet
(\mathcal{E},L,M)
where
L
,
M
are two Dirac structures of
\mathcal{E}
such that
\mathcal{E}=L\oplus M
is referred to as a
Manin triple
. Both the notions of Manin pairs and triples recover their usual extension in the context of Lie algebras when the base manifold
\mathscr{M}
is a point. Letting
\big({\mathcal{E}},L\big)
and
\big({\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},L^{\prime}\big)
be two Manin pairs over the same manifold
\mathscr{M}
, a morphism of Manin pairs
96
96

96

Similarly, letting
\big({\mathcal{E}},L,M\big)
and
\big({\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},L^{\prime}M^{\prime}\big)
be two Manin triples over the same manifold
\mathscr{M}
, a morphism of Manin triples is a morphism of Courant algebroids
\mathcal{F}\in\text{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
such that
\mathcal{F}(L)\subseteq L^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}(M)\subseteq M^{\prime}
.
is a morphism of Courant algebroids
\mathcal{F}\in\text{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
such that
\mathcal{F}(L)\subseteq L^{\prime}
.

Letting
\mathcal{E}=E\oplus E^{*}
be the pseudo-Euclidean vector bundle associated to the vector bundle
E
over
\mathscr{M}
and endowed with the canonical fiber-wise metric
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}
, we have the following hierarchy of identifications:

•

proto-Lie bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
Courant algebroids structures on
\big(\mathcal{E},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\big)
,

•

Lie-quasi bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
Manin pairs
\big((\mathcal{E},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}),E\big)
,

•

quasi-Lie bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
Manin pairs
\big((\mathcal{E},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}),E^{*}\big)
,

•

Lie bialgebroids on
(E,E^{*})

\Leftrightarrow
Manin triples
\big((\mathcal{E},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}),E,E^{*}\big)
.

Such identification allows to define morphisms of proto/Lie-quasi/quasi-Lie/Lie bialgebroids as morphisms of Courant algebroids preserving additional Dirac structures.
Explicitly, morphisms of pseudo-vector bundles
E\oplus E^{*}\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\longrightarrow}E^{\prime}\oplus E^{\prime}{}^{*}
with canonical fiber-wise metrics generically take the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(e)^{a^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}^{a^{\prime}}{}_{b}X^{b}+\mathcal{F}^{a^{\prime}b}\alpha_{b},\qquad\mathcal{F}(e)_{a^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{a^{\prime}b}X^{b}+\mathcal{F}_{a^{\prime}}{}^{b}\alpha_{b}

satisfying

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{c^{\prime}(a}\mathcal{F}^{c^{\prime}}{}_{b)}=0,\qquad\mathcal{F}^{c^{\prime}(a}\mathcal{F}_{c^{\prime}}{}^{b)}=0,\qquad\mathcal{F}_{c^{\prime}}{}^{a}\mathcal{F}^{c^{\prime}}{}_{b}+\mathcal{F}^{c^{\prime}a}\mathcal{F}_{c^{\prime}b}=\delta^{a}{}_{b}.

Such morphisms map the Dirac structure
E
to
E^{\prime}
if and only if
\mathcal{F}_{a^{\prime}b}=0
and the Dirac structure
E^{*}
to
E^{\prime}{}^{*}
if and only if
\mathcal{F}^{a^{\prime}b}=0
.
Infinitesimal endomorphisms of the pseudo-Euclidean vector bundle
(E\oplus E^{*},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\mathcal{E}})
read
97
97

97

Restricting to Lie-quasi (resp. quasi-Lie) bialgebroids yields
\omega\equiv 0
(resp.
\Lambda\equiv 0
), so that the subalgebra
\mathsf{End}\big({\Gamma(E)}\big)
acts on the abelian ideal
\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E\big)

(
resp.
\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E^{*}\big)
)
by rotations.

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{F}(e)=\big(\lambda(X)+\tfrac{1}{2}\iota_{\alpha}\Lambda\big)\oplus\big({-}\lambda^{T}(\alpha)+\tfrac{1}{2}\iota_{X}\omega\big),

where

•

\lambda\in\mathsf{End}({\Gamma(E)})
generates infinitesimal rotations of the fibers of
(E,E^{*})
,

•

\Lambda\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E\big)
generates the infinitesimal version of the twist of Lie-quasi bialgebroids (
A.7
),

•

\omega\in\Gamma\big({\wedge}^{2}E^{*}\big)
generates the infinitesimal version of the twist of quasi-Lie bialgebroids (
A.8
).

Appendix B
Incarnation of the
\boldsymbol{\Theta}
-graph in
\boldsymbol{d=3}

The present appendix is devoted to collect some additional results regarding the exotic
\mathsf{Lie}_{\infty}
-structure
\theta
of Theorem
1.1
generated by the cocycle class
[\Theta_{3}]\in H^{1}({\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}})
. For concreteness, we fix a representative of the class
[\Theta_{3}]
as follows:

Proposition B.1
.

There is a unique pair of combination of graphs
\Theta_{3}\in\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
and
\vartheta_{3}\in\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}^{\text{\rm black}}
such that:

1.

\Theta_{3}=-\delta\vartheta_{3}
, i.e.,
\Theta_{3}
is exact in
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}^{\text{\rm black}}
.

2.

\Theta_{3}
contains only graphs of the shape
C
, cf. Figure
8
.

3.

Each graph of
\vartheta_{3}
contains at least one red cycle, i.e.,
\vartheta_{3}\notin\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
.

Remark B.2
.

•

The combination of graphs
\vartheta_{3}
contains 68 black-oriented graphs (48 graphs of shape
A
and 20 graphs of shape
B
) while the combination
\Theta_{3}
contains 288 bi-oriented graphs of shape
C
.

•

Although
\Theta_{3}
is exact in
\mathsf{o}_{1}\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}^{\text{\rm black}}
, it is crucial to note that
\Theta_{3}
is
not
exact in
\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
, i.e., there is no combination of graphs
\kappa_{3}\in\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
such that
\Theta_{3}=-\delta\kappa_{3}
. Hence
\Theta_{3}
is a non-trivial cocycle in
\mathsf{o}_{2}\mathsf{d}_{0}\mathsf{fGC}_{3}
.

Figure 8:
Shape of graphs involved in
\vartheta_{3}
(
A
and
B
) and
\Theta_{3}
(
C
).

Let
E\overset{\pi}{\to}\mathscr{M}
be a vector bundle. To each Lie bialgebroid structure on
(E,E^{*})
(represented by the Hamiltonian function
{\mathscr{H}}\in\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
), we will associate the functions

•

\vartheta_{3}({\mathscr{H}}):={\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}_{5}(\vartheta_{3})({\mathscr{H}}^{\otimes 5})\in\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{Lie-quasi}}
,

•

\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}}):={\rm\mathsf{Rep}}^{E}_{6}(\Theta_{3})({\mathscr{H}}^{\otimes 6})\in\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
.

Note that the condition
\Theta_{3}=-\delta\vartheta_{3}
ensures that
\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})\sim{\mathsf{Q}}[\vartheta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})]
– where the differential
{\mathsf{Q}}
is defined as
{\mathsf{Q}}:=\big\{{{\mathscr{H}}},{\cdot}\big\}_{\Omega}^{E}
– so that
\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
is a coboundary in the complex
(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{Lie-quasi}}|{\mathsf{Q}})
. However,
\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
is generically not exact in
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
and the obstruction for
\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
to be a coboundary in
\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
is precisely given by the component of
\vartheta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
proportional to
\zeta^{3}
. We will denote this obstruction as
\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}
, so that

\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=0\ \Rightarrow\ \vartheta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})\in\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}

and
\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
is a trivial cocycle in
(\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\text{Lie-quasi}}|{\mathsf{Q}})
.
A straightforward computation gives

\displaystyle\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=R^{d|\mu}R^{e|\nu}\big(2\rho_{f}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{\mu}{C_{e}{}^{[a|f}}\partial_{\lambda\nu}{C_{d}{}^{bc]}}-\rho_{d}{}^{\beta}\partial_{\beta\nu}{R^{[a|\lambda}}\partial_{\mu\lambda}{C_{e}{}^{bc]}}-\rho_{d}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{\mu}{C_{e}{}^{[a|f}}\partial_{\lambda\nu}{C_{f}{}^{bc]}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=}{}-\partial_{\mu}{R^{f|\lambda}}f_{ef}{}^{[a}\partial_{\nu\lambda}{C_{d}{}^{bc]}}-2f_{df}{}^{[a}\partial_{\mu}{C_{e}{}^{b|g}}\partial_{\nu}{C_{g}{}^{c]f}}+2f_{fg}{}^{[a}\partial_{\mu}{C_{e}{}^{b|f}}\partial_{\nu}{C_{d}{}^{c]g}}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=}{}+R^{d|\mu}\partial_{\mu}{R^{e|\nu}}\big(f_{df}{}^{[a}C_{e}{}^{fg}\partial_{\nu}{C_{g}{}^{bc]}}-2f_{ef}{}^{[a}C_{d}{}^{fg}\partial_{\nu}{C_{g}{}^{bc]}}+\rho_{d}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}{C_{e}{}^{[a|f}}\partial_{\nu}{C_{f}{}^{bc]}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=}{}-2\rho_{e}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}{C_{d}{}^{[a|f}}\partial_{\nu}{C_{f}{}^{bc]}}-\rho_{e}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}{C_{f}{}^{[ab}}\partial_{\nu}{C_{d}{}^{c]f}}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=}{}+\rho_{d}{}^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}{R^{e|\nu}}R^{d|\mu}\partial_{\mu\nu}{R^{[a|\lambda}}\partial_{\lambda}{C_{e}{}^{bc]}}+2\rho_{e}{}^{\lambda}R^{d|\mu}\partial_{\lambda\mu}{R^{[a|\nu}}C_{d}{}^{ef}\partial_{\nu}{C_{f}{}^{bc]}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=}{}+\rho_{g}{}^{\nu}R^{d|\mu}C_{d}{}^{ef}\partial_{\nu}{C_{e}{}^{[ab}}\partial_{\mu}{C_{f}{}^{c]g}}.

The latter encodes the first obstruction for
{\mathscr{H}}
to define a “quantizable Lie bialgebroid”. Although the obstruction does not vanish for a generic Lie bialgebroid, the following proposition displays two important examples:

Proposition B.3
.

The obstruction vanishes for

•

Lie bialgebras,

•

coboundary Lie bialgebroids.

Proof.

Setting
R^{a|\mu}\equiv 0
yields
\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}=0
hence the obstruction vanishes for Lie bialgebras. More generally, it can be checked that each graph appearing in the combinations
\vartheta_{3}
and
\Theta_{3}
contains at least one arrow of the type
i
j
so that both
\vartheta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
and
\Theta_{3}({\mathscr{H}})
vanish identically on the graded Poisson subalgebra
98
98

98

See footnote
16
.

\mathcal{A}^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm Lie}\subset\mathcal{A}^{E}_{\rm Lie}
.

For coboundary Lie bialgebroids, we perform the replacement
R^{a|\mu}=\rho_{b}{}^{\mu}\Lambda^{ba}
and
C_{c}{}^{ab}=-\rho_{c}{}^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\Lambda^{ab}-2\Lambda^{d[a}f_{dc}{}^{b]}
, with
\Lambda^{ab}=\Lambda^{[ab]}
a bivector, see Appendix
A
. Under this replacement, it can be checked that
\text{\rm Ob}({\mathscr{H}})^{abc}
identically vanishes modulo the defining conditions
\mathcal{C}_{1}\equiv 0
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}\equiv 0
ensuring that the maps
(\rho,f)
define a Lie algebroid.
∎
